Luke L.
Elite 26
All-Star
San Gabriel, CA
39 Italian Food reviews
Nov 12, 2023
10 photos
I waited for over a decade, but I finally had a chance to visit the #1 most reviewed restaurants and food businesses on Yelp in the United States (https://blog.yelp.com/community/the-top-50-most-reviewed-restaurants-and-eateries-on-yelp/). Bottega Louie is a restaurant / gourmet market / patisserie and café located in Downtown Los Angeles. The restaurant is one of the most photogenic and popular restaurant in Downtown Los Angeles. Bottega Louie has another location in West Hollywood.

[Parking]: $20 valet parking and expect to wait 10 minutes to retrieve car. The parking garage across the street (725 S Grand Ave) offers $12 daily max from Monday to Friday before 4 PM ($2.00 every 15 minutes) and $7 in evening after 4 PM. Flat rate $7 on Saturday and Sunday. If you are lucky enough then it is cheaper to park via street metered parking (free metered parking on Sunday). The adjacent Whole Foods parking lot offers free 90 minute parking with validation at Whole Food, but park at your own risk as (1) one time I validated my parking ticket to shop at Whole and Foods and I still ended up paying for my parking ticket and (2) the lot is for Whole Foods customers only.

[Reservation]: The restaurant / cafe eliminated no-reservations policy and customers are now able to book a reservation through Resy. I actually booked a same-day 12:15 PM reservation 75 minutes ahead of the scheduled time.

[Service]: Pretty standard service as expected from an upscale brunch spot like this. Please note that the restaurant automatically adds 5% surcharge to mitigate inflation & other operating costs (it is not a service charge, gratuity, or paid to employees).

[Menu]: Yelp highlighted brunch items include Smoked Salmon Benedict / Eggs Benedict, Portobello Mushroom, Burrata Pizza, and French Toast. Items like Trenne Pasta, Pork Chop, and Braised Prime Rib Eye are only available during dinner hours. Lastly don't forget to take a look at their colorful macarons near the front entrance.

***

Smoked Salmon Benedict (Poached eggs, smoked salmon, crème fraîche, red onions, sautéed spinach & hollandaise sauce on hash brown potato pancakes - $32): This dish was alright and all within expectations - hash brown didn't really enhanced the overall texture for poached eggs and smoked salmon.

Burrata (Mozzarella, burrata, prosciutto di Parma, pecorino romano, sauteed rapini, minced garlic & extra virgin olive oil - 14-inch pizza $36): Amazing cheese and this pizza is definitely a must-order. It was enough portion to share among 3 full sized adults.

This is a perfect restaurant to have a wonderful brunch or dinner with a date; however I am not a fan of 5% "inflation" surcharge as each item on the brunch menu is already pretty expensive. Delicious pizza, decent service, and average benedict. I can't fault anyone for visiting this foodie trap with the spectacular decor and eye-catching baked goods section.

I've had this place bookmarked for a while and finally made a reservation to go with my family. You make reservations through their website on Resy or directly on Resy's website. We had a pepperoni pizza, burrata pizza, and hamburger with fries. We also had their Italian sodas. The pizzas are so good! The pepperoni comes with mozzarella, pecorino romano, San Marzano tomato sauce, Sicilian oregano, & extra virgin olive oil. The Burrata comes with burrata, prosciutto di parma, garlic sauteed rapini, & extra virgin olive oil. The hamburger comes with grass feed beef, bibb lettuce, grilled onions, tomato, and basil aioli on a brioche bun with fries. My son loved his burger. He didn't add the onions or tomatoes, but the basil aioli gave it a great taste. And the fries remind me of McDonald's fries a little bit but better. Oh and the complimentary bread with butter was so good. I forgot to ask what butter they use, but it was super yummy. Our server was so nice and attentive. Italian is my favorite cuisine, and Bottega Louie didn't disappoint.

The restaurant is conveniently located next to 2 parking lots and on the same block as Whole Foods, so you won't have to panic about parking (which is often impossible downtown). The ambiance is very elegant and classy.

For our lunch, my friend and I enjoyed the Calamari Fritti, Blue Prawns Bagna Cauda, Avocado Toast, and ended with an assortment of their famous macarons.

I usually pass on the table bread, but the smell of fresh bread from the next table made me reconsider. The bread is delicious! I started with the calamari fritti, which was fried to perfection! The texture has to be perfect. I don't like over-fried calamari. It was perfect, and beyond delicious. The avocado toast was so big that we shared! 10/10! Lastly, my favorite dish, the Blue Prawns Bagna Cauda (DELICIOUS!!!) These prawns were enormous, perfectly seasoned, and fried to perfection! Flavorful and delectable.

We ended with the macarons, which were so delicious that I ordered a set of them to take home. They looked too beautiful to eat!

The service was phenomenal, and I will surely be returning!

We came here on a late saturday afternoon after the NBA All Stars Jam Session. I have always pass by this place and noticed it was always packed with people. It is quite loud in here and seems like a happening spot in downtown. They have a bunch of pastries. My boyfriend really enjoyed the drinks here, so if you're looking for a good drink, I'd give this place a try.

The DISH:
Farmers market breakfast: this wasn't bad. the poach eggs were really good and the tomatoes were delicious. It's kinda wet though. But you can tell the ingredients are fresh, the quality of the mushrooms and green beans were really up to par.
Lemon Ricotta pancakes with blueberry syrup: this is a nice simple dish topped with powdered sugar and garnished with blueberries. It wasn't too sweet and had a tanginess but the ricotta balances the flavors. My only thing to improve on this is that it needs to be moister.
The french fries here were fair. It is something I would not order again though.
Arugula salad: I liked the chunks or parmesan cheese. I would go easy on the olive oil though.
Napoli pizza: good crust.
Clam pizza: MOST DELICIOUS THING I HAD. It has a great garlic and clam flavor. I would recommend and order this again and again. Will not disappoint especially if you like seafood.

We also had a great server. All in all, I would come here again if I was in the area.

All-Star
Austin, TX
54 Brunch reviews
Jun 10, 2023Updated review
10 photos
Overall: 4.6/5 (dine in)

My friends and I came for coffee and pastries which had a different seating area from the restaurant. They had a great variety of pastries, desserts, and drinks for a reasonable price.

Zeppoli ($5): This is an Italian pastry consisting of a deep-fried dough topped with sugar. It's very flakey, and you get that crunch from the granular sugar. However, it was more on the bland side so I didn't care for it.

Pretzel Cheese ($4.50): This is very different from the pretzels I had at Auntie Anne's or Wetzel's Pretzels. You barely taste the cheese so I'm not sure what I was tasting. I didn't like it but this was my friend's favorite.

Monkey Bread ($5.50): This is an explosion of flavors because it has so many things in there - you get cinnamon, chocolate, & walnut all from one bite. I think it would have been better warm instead of room temperature but it was definitely one of my favorites. A very close first to the chocolate hazelnut supreme croissant.

Chocolate Hazelnut Supreme Croissant ($9): Had to get this since I've been seeing this all over TikTok, and this was my favorite pastry. I love the croissant taste and texture. It had condensed layers so it's not as flakey as the zeppoli but it tasted better. The chocolate hazelnut topping and filling wasn't too sweet but I think there was a bit too much of the chocolate filling. If we didn't split this between the 3 of us, I would say the chocolate would have been overwhelming and the monkey bread would have been my favorite.

16 Oz Iced Lavender Latte With Oat Milk ($6.25): Wow, this was really good. The barista gave me the latte with the layers all separated instead of mixed up. I barely saw a thin line of the lavender syrup so I thought the flavor would be light but I was wrong. The latte was well balanced and delicious!

95 Brunch reviews
May 14, 2023Updated review
2 photos
This is THE place to be in the morning with a drink and pastry in hand... but I went at night because work obligations call first, unfortunately. Even though I went late in the day, they still had a variety of pastries left of which the raspberry beignet and monkey bread caught my eye.

Monkey bread is such a nostalgic childhood snack for me and this one was a much more eloquent version than the one my mom always made. It had a beautiful flaky outer crust with the most buttery, sweet cinnamon-y center. The pull-apart aspect is my favorite part of the pastry and with every pull, there was a mixture of pecans and chocolate chunks.

The raspberry beignet was like your typical doughnut with a sugary dusting and raspberry filling. There wasn't really a wow factor.

My drink of choice was the Matcha Nitro Latte which I'd never heard of before. The kind lady explained to me that it was ceremonial matcha concentrate with nitrogen-infused cold brew. The result was a really light, airy drink that I honestly could've drank in just 3 sips. We got one regular and one with lavender syrup which I highly recommend. The floral note adds a great depth of flavor to the fluffy matcha drink.

I had such a great experience here overall with both customer service and ambiance. I love natural light and the window seating was perfect. With a large, luxurious pastry selection and a unique drink menu, this is a great place to catch up with friends.

Fluffy raspberry beignet

Monkey bread with dark chocolate chunks and pecans

May 6, 2023Previous review
This was not the first choice restaurant to celebrate my boyfriend's birthday but wow am I glad it popped up on Yelp.

I was craving something with tomato sauce so I went with the margherita pizza despite my usual love for white sauce. The crust was AMAZING. The center is very thin and crispy while the crust is so doughy and fluffy. The combination and execution could not be more perfect...and this is coming from someone who generally does not like pizza.

We also got carbonara which was creamy and slightly on the saltier side. I didn't mind it though because I ended up using some of my crust to eat with the pasta and that combination was immaculate. I loved that the pancetta came in thick pieces and the poached egg provided a good amount of creaminess.

To end the night, we ordered a blueberry mascarpone pastry which reminded me of the Great British Baking Show because I know how labor intensive and delicate these desserts are to make. The exterior was beautiful and the interior was delicious. The packaging and all the small designs were so carefully detailed, I couldn't help but want to keep all of it even after we finished eating.

The interior is very spacious with small, intimate tables and large tables to accommodate bigger parties. Our waitress was very kind and contributed to the great experience that we had overall.

Andrew D.
Los Angeles, CA
50 Brunch reviews
May 14, 2024
2 photos
Yeah, the hype is real. As someone who came in with no expectations aside from the word-of-mouth from macaron aficionados on social media and friends, I was pleasantly surprised with how, well, excellent everything operates at Bottega Louie. From the flexibility with the waiting window for reservations (traffic and parking on any given night is a nightmare in DTLA), the fast service, to the quality of the food--Bottega Louie's team brings their A-game to the table.

What I tried:

Nitro Oat Milk Draft Latte: Nitro Cold Brew concentrate, oat milk, agave sweetener. Solid coffee beverage.

Lasagna: Best dish from the lot; meaty ragu, an array of Italian cheeses and cream sauces (Béchamel), layered between thin pasta. A hearty dish.

Burrata Pizza: Burrata, Mozzarella, Prosciutto, garlic, drizzled with olive oil.

Bianco Pizza: Mozzarella, ricotta, garlic, Parmigiano Reggiano, and arugula. I prefer the Burrata solely because the Bianco goes heavy-handed on the arugula; a delicious pizza if you don't mind an abundance of arugula that overpowers the other ingredients between bites.

And...what Bottega Louie is known for...macarons. Call me uncultured, uncouth, and downright unadventurous--I had never tried a macaron before my visit to Bottega Louie; I'm the "Go big or go home," type of guy when it comes to desserts, and macarons always seemed so petite and unsatisfying to me. Well, I actually enjoyed the chocolate macaron I purchased on my way out the door--small but hit the spot!

For all those of you that are adverse to dining at seemingly overhyped or overtly expensive restaurants, give Bottega Louie a go; you'll get both quantity and quality along with some ritzy pictures for your social media page!

The Lasagna. As good as it looks.

Half Bianco, Half Burrata.

Bottega Louie has become THE place that I'll have to go when I'm ever in LA again! We stopped by here for lunch during weekday, and since we arrived early, we were able to get seated right away. The restaurant became super busy as lunch time approaches. Pro Tip: make a reservation via Resy to ensure you get a table. I didn't know they do reservation until I got there, so I'll make sure to have a reservation for future reference.

Bottega Louie has 2 sections: the front part is the bakery and the restaurant is in the back. The space is very bright, spacious and has super high ceilings. It does get loud when it gets busy and you kinda have to shout to talk to each other.

Service was good. Attentive, nothing to complain about. Now onto the food: we ordered sausage pizza, carbonara pasta and had the le Noir as dessert. The Carbonara was the star of the lunch and it was sooooo good! The pizza was good too, but not as good as the pasta. We finished our meal with the Le Noir cake, and omg it was so good! I can say that it's probably the best chocolate cake I've had.

Overall, a very pleasant experience with great food. If I'm ever in LA again, I'd definitely come back!

Meh, I was a little disappointed but the experience.

We started our night at the bar, while waiting for our table. The drinks were nicely made and tasted good. However, the bar top was not maintained very well. There was some trash on the counter that was not cleaned up the whole time we were there. I honestly got a sense that the whole place was not very well taken care of. It like, it WANTS to seem upscale but when you look closer, you see trash and dirt and dust.

We got seated and we ordered the ibérico appetizer. The ibérico was three, small pieces. It was paired with bruschetta. The bruschetta was plain, plain, plain. It only consisted of chopped tomato. No oil, no salt, not nothin'. It was try bread with dry tomatoes on top (that weren't even delicious on their own.). The ibérico was served cold, so the fat was firm. The appeal of ibérico is to have it warmed up a bit, so the fat melts a little bit and it melts in your mouth as you chew. It came with a side of olives that were good, but seemed unnecessary. The whole dish was off. Just plate some ibérico and manchego cheese, with some olives if you want. The bruschetta was an odd addition.

My friend ordered the braised short rib in a reduction sauce and polenta. I though the dish was nice and humble. The flavors complimented each other and the meat was tender.

I ordered the truffle pizza. It was okay...

The appetizer was disappointing. The meals were good but I wasn't smitten.

Pranjali S.
Elite 26
San Diego, CA
95 Brunch reviews
May 14, 2023
10 photos
We were lucky to get seated immediately after showing up on a fairly busy Sat during the USC graduation and Mother's Day weekend. I couldn't make reservations online so I assume they didn't have a reservation system.

When you walk in, you get a full view of their bakery section, the delicious macarons and pasties on the right and the open view kitchen on the left. The whole place has a relaxed, modern and fancy-casual feel. It is definitely loud, thanks to the open layout. So you do have to scream into people's ears. The service was very nice but could've been a little more prompt.

We ordered plates to share so we all tasted a bit of everything - the lobster hash, the smoked salmon Benedict, the calamari fritti and the Louis salad. I got an iced matcha green tea latte which was very very good for a matcha lover.

The smoked salmon Benedict was the best of all the food. The salmon had a slight tarty-spicy kick to it which I enjoyed. The hash cake it lay on was also delicious. The hollandaise was well done as was the poached egg. All in all, great recipe.

The lobster hash sounded much better than it was. The hash was delicious. The lobster could've had a little more salt or spice to it as it was pretty bland. The sauce it came in was too liquid and had a yolky taste to it which I didn't particularly love. Some of the greens accompanying the dish were also little bitter for my taste buds. So, a dish that sounded tastier than it was.

The calamari was as expected. Nothing too fancy, nothing too out of the ordinary. It was a nice accompaniment as a smaller plate with 3 other big plates.

The Louis salad was delicious! As a rule, I don't order salads because I don't consider it a full meal. But this salad was a tasty medley of greens, crab, avocadoes and a dressing that got my taste buds singing. It was also a huge portion! Among the four of us, we had a hard time finishing everything.

As a dessert, we first ordered the almond pistachio cherry pastry which deserves an entire star in itself. It looked so so perfect and, if it is even possible, tasted better than it looked! The pistachio jaconde cake was moist and light, the cherry marmalade was perfectly sweet and firm and the almond and pistachio mousse was so so tasty. We cleaned that up!

We then also ordered a limonchello which was a tarty tart with a limonchello curd and limonchello cloud with pralines. Now, as a rule, I'm one of those tart-loving people who complain "not enough lemon, needs more lime..." Well, ladies and gentlemen, not this time! This was so so tarty and just the right level of sweet that I couldn't get enough of it! The tart itself was a little firm and we were so full, I only licked off all the limoncello curd and the cloud. I'm not a fan of pralines, especially when they interfere with my soul-calming experience with limonchello or lemon or lime tartness. So maybe they should get rid of the pralines.

All in all, the food is good but those pastries blew out minds. Come here for the pastries. I wish I could've tried the macarons but we were so full that we could not. So next time it is!

Iris L.
Elite 26
Alhambra, CA
21 Brunch reviews
Jul 4, 2023
9 photos
I have been wanting to check this place out for a while, and finally made time for it recently. The place is pretty spacious, not a lot of decor in the dining room.

Originally, I thought it's a chic place for a quiet brunch, it turns out to be the opposite. There's no music played in the dining area. All I heard about were people talking around me. I happened to be sitting on the bench area and I felt every step when the little kid was jumping next to me. While I was waiting for my server, I had nothing to do but to look at other people which were sitting on the other side of the room, which was awkward. The tables were a bit closer than what I would've liked. As far as the food goes, I ordered the pancake, lobster hash and portebello fries.

Lobster hash- comes with 2 poached eggs with lobster on top, surrounded by a sauce similar to lobster bisque sand hash at the bottom. The lobster is not overcooked or rubbery, which is nice.

Le pancake- it's a nice souffle pancake with strawberry at the bottom, with strawberry sauce on the side. I like the fluffy texture and small amount of crispiness on the edges. I would definitely get it again if I ever come back.

Montebello fries- it's portebello mushroom with a batter and you dip it in the garlic aioli. I am uncertain what the hype is about for the fries. The batter for the fries is pretty thick, almost reminds me of potato wedges. It's good but not that great.

One thing that I didn't like was the fact that they added a 5% surcharge due to inflation. The restaurant could've just printed new menus and reflected the cost rather than add an additional charge at the end of the tap. Then on top of that, customers still have to tip. My brunch for 2 turned out to be a bit over $100, which is on the pricer side for brunch.

I also ordered an apple turnover and 2 macaroons to go. The apple turnover was pretty sweet, sweeter than my liking. I ordered the earl grey and coffee macaroon. I don't really like the earl grey since it has chocolate in the middle. The coffee one is pretty good

This is probably the best coffee date place you can find in downtown LA....maybe in all of LA even!

I was there on a weekday at 11:00 AM. There was lots of seating. Around noon, the place was packed, so if you're just going there for coffee and some baked goods, I'd recommend avoiding the lunch hours.

Since the floors are hard, it gets pretty noisy as more people arrive, but that's OK because they can't ear hustle you while you're talking to your friend!

There's also a restaurant and it had a very large lunch crowd. I hear the food is excellent. Maybe I'll try it sometime.

Oh, I just ordered tea and my friend had a latte'. It was about $15 with a tip.

Gretchen K.
Elite 26
Kissimmee, FL
47 Brunch reviews
Feb 20, 2023
2 photos
This location has been on a shared bucket list with a good friend of mine, as it is the flagship store and many celebrities have been seen dining here. (Prior to the pandemic, my friend recalled a fan or photographer catching BLACKPINK's Jennie and Red Velvet's Irene enjoying a meal here and playfully fighting over the bill.)

After a fun morning at Melrose Trading Post, my friend and I, plus my dad, came here for brunch. My friend arrived before we did and put our name in. (The wait was 5 minutes max before we were seated.) Please note if you come before 3 PM on the weekends, the brunch menu is served through to 3 PM and they cannot accommodate their regular menu. (They have some of their staples like a few pasta dishes and pizzas, but you won't find the full offering on the brunch menu.)

There are two choices for parking - either you can do valet for the restaurant which is a flat fee of $30 or you can find a lot or garage near the restaurant and park and walk. Both my friend and I did the latter and found a structure across the street that charged $7 flat fee for the day. The walk to the restaurant was very short - go up to one of the crosswalks and cross, then go up to the restaurant on the corner.

Staff were polite and very on top of service - they would notice empty plates or when you were finished with something and would clear it for space on your table.

My friend ordered the carbonara, I ordered a pizza, and my dad got an eggs Benedict. We shared the beignets at the table.

The beignets were dusted in sugar and came with a tart raspberry sauce to dip/drizzle on top. The flavors balanced out nicely and kept the beignet from skewing too sweet or heavy.

Food came out a couple of minutes after we finished the beignets. They make the pizzas fresh in one of the hot ovens and we were sitting near one of the areas where they were making them. (It was fun watching our food from start to finish.)

Their sauce is good and they were generous with the toppings. The crust has a good crunch but I will admit with the sauce and thin crust that it could have the potential to become soggy at times if you didn't eat it right away.

My friend enjoyed her carbonara and my dad liked the Benedict.

I will admit this location is very different aesthetically from the one in West Hollywood. This one has more of an upscale, classic architecture with visible kitchen spaces and often it becomes quite noisy in there with everyone talking at once. Tables are put together a bit closer than the West Hollywood one so I would say this one feels more close in terms of how they packed parties in and maybe at times a little crowded. In contrast, West Hollywood was more relaxed but still had a balance of elegance and contemporary elements in the space. (Also it can be a bit of a tourist destination for people who hear about the restaurant or saw their favorite celebrities dine here once.)

I noticed the flagship didn't have as many flavors of their macarons out as they did at West Hollywood. It seems like this one was focusing more on the cakes and pastries.

Prices are mid to high - I would say this is a fun experience to do once in a while, but unless you have the means to do it more often, it can be a little pricey.

Pepperoni Pizza

Earl Grey tea and Beignets

Came here on a Wednesday morning around 9am (weird time I know!). Inside ambiance was beautiful and bright. There was no wait so we were seated quickly. Ordered the salmon benedict ($30) and the Croque Madame ($20) to split with a friend. The salmon benedict was cooked nicely, and I enjoyed the hash brown that replaced the English muffin (a nice crunchy twist to an eggs benedict!). The Croque Madame was also tasty and was a large portion size. Both dishes came with a salad. Service was slow towards the end as it took a while to get the check and they stopped refilling our waters.

I got the raspberry, strawberry, and mango macaroons to go for my bf (3 macaroons were $10). I tried a few bites of the mango one and it was so good! Perfect texture, and a good amount of filling inside.

This place is on the pricey side (plus there was a 5% surcharge), but quality of the food was great.

Always consistent! I live in Ventura county so I don't eat here as often as I like, BUT when I do it's just as good as when I first started coming here (7-8 years ago?).
Love their pizzas, we had the burrata AND the pepperoni. My personal favorite was the burrata-flavorful and fresh! Pizza dough was outstanding-chewy (not too thick) OR cracker thin and crunchy.
Everything is always SO FRESH here...the ingredients on the pizza, the salad and the bruschetta (another dish I'd order again). If you love bruschetta you must order this one! Tomatoes were flavorful, fresh & seasoned perfectly!
As always, we ordered some macaroons-espresso, salted caramel, lemon*, and ultimate chocolate*.
The drinks were spot on the last two times I've been.
The espresso martini was yummy, creamy and just the right amount of froth. The time before this I had a pear drink which was just as outstanding.

P.s. if you're not from the LA area make sure you plan out the parking situation. It'll save you time and frustration.

We were a bit worried that this place would be overhyped, but this was NOT the case at ALL! One of the best brunch spots we've tried EVER.

We tried the smoked salmon benedict, french toast, le pancake, avocado toast, and their freshly squeezed OJ. Though it is pretty expensive for brunch, the quality and taste of the food was totally worth it. Out of everything we tried, the french toast, avocado toast, and OJ is a must-try. Everything is pretty basic, but each dish was so well-executed that you NEED to come here at least once. We came for Saturday brunch but we were able to be seated right away. The restaurant itself is gorgeous and it's a great view/ambiance to take in while dining. The service was amazing as well; our waiter was beyond attentive and friendly which really enhanced our whole experience. Be sure to also grab some pastries on the way out; the worker who was helping us even snuck in an extra earl gray macaron in our box!

Though we visited from NYC, we will be sure to come back whenever we are in the area.

I was interested in trying Bottega Louie while visiting DTLA. I had a strange experience that worked out after all but definitely but a damper on my experience.

Since breakfast stops at 11 am I ordered ahead of time over the phone and informed them I would be coming to the restaurant. The person I spoke with on the phone said if I decided to stay I could eat my food at the restaurant. However, there was a complete misunderstanding when I arrived. I picked up my food and asked to be seated in the main dining area because I was planning to wait for a friend and enjoy my meal while I waited.

A petite female hostess insisted that I sit in the cafe area up front. But, I said, I would like to sit in the main area because I had a to-go-order. I explained I would like to get a plate for my food because I plan to sit and enjoy my meal and wait for a friend who will be ordering lunch. Eventually, her male colleague appeased me and led me to a seat. I mean, it was a whole ordeal, which made me annoyed about the non-obvious rules they seemed to have in place.

Finally, I got my seat and ate my breakfast sandwich. Once my friend arrived they order the Margharita and a side of fries for us to share.

Sometime standing up for yourself is work but at least I stood my ground and could eventually relax.

Chrystina B.
Elite 26
Los Angeles, CA
31 Brunch reviews
Nov 18, 2022
10 photos
THE BEST DESSERT, THE WORST PARKING, 5% CHARGE ON TOP OF BILL, BEAUTIFUL DECOR!

Oh lord, I have been wanting to try this place for quite sometime, and i am so glad I did!

Customer service: 10/10
For being a more upscale type of place, the people are so nice. To the woman who was seating people, to the bar tenders, and the cooks, Everyone was so kind. I appreciate that more than ever!

Food: 20/10
FRUIT TART--After dinner, (at Wokano) we stopped here to get my dessert (what is date night without a dessert, right?!) and I ordered quickly because the bartender said they were sold out of just about everything. He said he only had one fruit tart left and I took that so fast! Y'all....it was the best fruit tart I've ever had! Think of real Europe cheesecake, meets fresh picked fruit, meets fresh apple pie crust, meets a chef who said I'm going to get it right or else! Man, did he ever. It was not overly sweet, crunchy perfect fruit, and the texture was light and fluffy...just amazing. I cannot wait to go back and get another.

Price: 6/10
I love good food, but dang. I think $16.10 was a bit much for a small dessert. Like, Costco would give me a whole cake (lol)
The price is $14.00 then tax, then a 5% add on fee for workers. I wish restaurants paid their workers fair prices without having to charge us for it. The money they must make here is absolutely insane, so I pray they use it to pay their workers vs upping our fees. But that may be a different conversation for a different day.

All around: 8.5/10
The parking is hell on earth, the prices are a bit high, and the dessert does not miss. . I can cannot lie, I just might be back sooner than I think! If you can get past the traffic, parking, and prices, you'll just love it here too!!! :)

10 Brunch reviews
Sep 15, 2024
I'm really disappointed to write this review because I use to love Bottega Louie, but after moving out of the area for a few years and coming back for the first time last night I had a bad experience. We came in around 10pm to get drinks at the bar. First thing we noticed was bags of trash behind the bar, like someone was cleaning up but didn't finish taking the trash out. And we noticed it because there was a bad stench of trash.
Secondly, the staff was not welcoming or friendly at all. They were being short and snide with everyone like we just walked in right at closing. It took awhile for the bartender to acknowledge us even though the bar wasn't very crowded. Another patron noticed the state of disarray behind the bar and sensed the bartender's attitude. Thinking they may have been about to close the patron politely asked what time they close and the bartender said they closed at 12 am. Closing wasn't for another 2 hours! Yet the bar was left messy and the bartender was giving haughty attitude to everyone like we were hindering their closing operations.
Also, the drinks were 20 dollars BEFORE tax and tip. On top of that they added on some surcharge that was not a tip or tax, just a surcharge they added on top and they expected you to still tip and pay tax. We wanted to ask about the surcharge but the bartender had disappeared for a really long time after running our credit card so we eventually just signed the check and left.
I had a pear martini which was actually pretty good so I guess that's one good thing, but for that much I expect at least some decent service and a bar that doesn't smell like trash, this isn't a dive bar.
I'm hoping their macarons are still as good as they use to be, but as for the bar at night you're better off elsewhere.

Julie P.
Elite 26
All-Star
Baltimore, MD
26 Brunch reviews
Mar 10, 2023
8 photos
Super cute Pâtisserie and restaurant. Everything was so aesthetically pleasing and I was surprised to see the flower arrangements we saw everywhere including the bathroom was real!

We came for lunch and had the carbonara, Trenne Pasta, tartufo (truffle) pizza, and the sea bass. Everything was delicious. The carbonara was one of the best I've had and the trenne pasta was fried and had a very interesting crisp and chewiness to it. The tartufo pizza was very interesting and is yummy if you're a truffle fan. Otherwise I can see it coming off as having a very strong truffle taste which could be difficult to eat for some. Our pizza actually was forgotten in the kitchen when it was made and so a new pizza was made and came out a little late (maybe 15 mins) and to our surprise it got taken off the check as an apology. The service here is so great and staff was so friendly and helpful.

In the front of the restaurant is the bakery with very beautifully made pastries, cakes, and sweets. We got some to go & they were put in a gorgeous pastry box and bagged for us. I enjoyed the experience and would definitely come again!

Imperative to stop in if you're in DTLA. I came in twice over my weeklong stay. I just bought to-go treats from the case; the first time I got a nutella croissant type thing and the second a handful of macarons.

It would be fun to eat here and despite the fame and name, the prices seemed reasonable (for LA) and I saw all kinds of people there from the casual to a little more dressed up.

You won't be alone, but they hustle people in and out. With over 18K reviews and (I think?) this being the MOST PHOTOGRAPHED business on Yelp, you can peruse the pics and reviews and know exactly what you want beforehand in order to get you on your way quicker. I recommend getting there early for the good baked stuff in the far case, otherwise by evening you just have a few of the more basic dessert case options.

Dec 8, 2018Previous review
The cool thing about LA is that you can walk into a place like this in your PJs and no one will bat an eyelash because for all they know, you're a billionaire tech guru or a rapper. But let's face it, you are neither of these, nor do you belong wearing PJs to a restaurant like this. But hey, who am I to judge you, especially because I'm not a rapper or a tech guru and I went very underdressed.

The waitstaff is attractive and the host staff are very polite. They have over 200 seats so there are no reservations. Your entire party has to be there and then they will seat you or if there is a wait, put your names on the list.

The cocktail choices are very inventive. I went for the mule and wasn't disappointed.

Our server was a complete angel and despite having numerous tables, was able to keep up with us and we never once felt like we were waiting or that she was a ghost.

The food is spectacular considering I haven't doing GREAT Italian in LA. The bolognese and the pomodoro are solid.

The desert choices are endless. That's the first thing you'll notice because they have a huge display in the front.

Overall I'd definitely go back. If you dont like the food, there's all kinds of rappers and tech billionaires there so you'll definitely enjoy the people watching.

The most incredible place I almost didn't experience! I was visiting DTLA for an event at Crypto Arena. The weather turned bad and I searched the map to see what local food options were available. Stumbled upon this magical place that would've fit in well on SATC during its heyday.

To start, Customer Service is fantastic. I was literally greeted and ushered in off the street by an employee who witnessed the confusion on face as to where to enter the massive building. Also to be fair, their attire caught my attention and I was entranced with that as well. Once I realized the connection, I immediately loved how committed this place was to creating an experience.

I didn't make a reservation but was able to get immediate seating and by a window no less (insert brownie points).

I loved that food was prepared out in the open. I had a view of the pizza station with the beautiful brick oven and the fire in the back. A very elegant touch that kept me thinking of how great a date spot this place is.

I ordered the best Lasagna I've ever tasted, which took the sting off the price. And followed up with a dessert that would be fit for royalty. If there was ever a time to not let cost get in the way of a fabulous meal, this is that moment.

My only con is that I didn't have someone else along to take pictures of me in this gorgeous place

Elite 26

75 Brunch reviews

I really enjoyed my brunch here. It was super close to our hotel and looked so bright and lovely though it's surroundings were dtla. Maybe I am getting soft from living in paradise though. Anyhow we walk into this huge bright airy space with a bakery in the front. The macarons and cakes were gorgeous colors, almost like jewels in a case.

They do take reservations which my cousin had wisely made in advance. The noise level was high bc of the space so it was a little hard to hear conversation. The bathrooms were really clean and nice and they have the fancy thick paper towels to dry your hands!

The food was really nice - my fave was the portobello fries. I guess I didn't know what to expect but these were gigantic sticks covered in a thin crunchy batter as big as a zucchini stick! The sauce they give you is some kind of aioli which was a perfect accompaniment. Also got the arugula fennel salad which was refreshing and not overdressed. The burrata pizza also yummy and I'm still enjoying the leftovers. The broccoli rabe is seasoned with a little red pepper.

The best dessert was the napoleon - the cream is delicious and not overly sweet and the pastry is flaky and crunchy. The chocolate eclair was fine. More beautiful and delicious. How do they get the glaze so shiny and smooth?

Our server Kerry was super friendly, competent, attentive. Overall a great experience. 5% kitchen fee or whatever people are calling it lately so don't be surprised. It's not part of the gratuity.

Mar 27, 2019Previous review
One of my favorite restaurants in DTLA. The restaurant is classy, beautiful and spacious. I love the high ceilings and the open kitchen. They also have a pizza station in the back where you can see the pizza being made and baked in the huge oven.
When you walk in you see the beautiful display of macaroons and desserts. Variety of gift items on the wall shelves. All the desserts are picture perfect creations. They taste heavenly and are surprisingly priced reasonably. So many flavors of macaroons to chose from and the beautiful boxes they come in. Great for gifts or just to spoil yourself.
The dining area gets very busy and loud. But the delicious food they serve makes up for that. The Margherita pizza is a must for me when I'm here. So delicious and flavorful. The Caesar salad is another must have. All the dishes I tried so far have been delicious.
The service is always good, starting from the host to the busboy, they make you feel welcomed and special. Very polite and friendly staff.
Overall a great place to eat and celebrate special occasions.

Came here today for the first time. I have had this place bookmarked for awhile. I don't go to Los Angeles all that much, so since I was in-town, we decided to stop by.

Arrived around 5pm or so. There weren't too many people there. The wait was 10 minutes, not bad.

We were seated in the far back corner, which was perfect. It was away from the windows so the sunlight wouldn't blind us. Or me anyway. I'm a total vampire.

Service was good. Our waitress was nice. She came and checked on us often.

Our food, it was good. But I think I expected more for how popular this place is. The pizza was alright. Pasta about the same as the pizza. I probably wouldn't go out of my way to come here again. It was nice to try once. The decor was cute and the restaurant was a pretty decent size. There were booths and tables available. We got a booth which I was happy about.

It did start to get crowded later around 8pm. But we were leaving by that time.

Overall, very good.

50 Brunch reviews
Oct 4, 2022
8 photos
Sweet Tooth: On Saturday after dinner & dessert in DTLA, my friends took me to one of my favorite places, Bottega Louie, for more dessert to go! Yum! Bottega Louie is a beautiful & elegant restaurant, even has a museum-like pristine vibe. Love the lighting as well. The bakery section reminds me of a glossy boutique!

Awe-Inspiring: The love, creativity & dedication that goes into making each & every colorful & edible creation of Bottega Louie's dessert rocks my entire world & shines in every single bite! It often takes a while to decide on which sweet treat to select, because everything is so beautiful, often shiny & even styled with gorgeous flowers!

Play It Again, Sam: While I have tried & enjoyed each dessert I've tried, my favorite continues to be the Raspberry-filled Grand Macaron, full-on yum! Since it was no longer available, as we arrived late night, I selected a slice of the shiny red Raspberry Chocolate Cake, which was yummy as well.

Food Memories/Larger Le Grand Macaron: Please bring back! It's the size of a cake! It was love at first sight & bite when one of my friends gifted me Le Grand Raspberry Macaron during the Christmas holidays in 2015. Family had flown in & friends had arrived for a feast & to play the White Elephant game!

Hide & Seek: The moment I took a forkful of the oversized Raspberry Le Grand Macaron, with the raspberries & fluffy crème inside, I knew at once that I had to hide it (And I did!), because it would be gone in 60 seconds! Haha! It's been love ever since...until I no longer saw it on the menu...

Overall: The dessert is a must!! I love the Margherita Pizza & Portobello Fries as well & it's always fun selecting one of the colorful macarons, as the flavors are plentiful! Love the entire experience with every visit to Bottega Louie! Still dreaming of the return of the Larger Raspberry Le Grand Macaron...!

The crossaint itself was huge and had such a tasty filling. The complimentary bread and butter were okay. I thought the bread was a bit stiff to eat and the butter wasn't easy to spread. Overall, skip the restaurant but the pastries are a must try!

Hannah L.
Elite 26
Dallas, TX
13 Brunch reviews
Dec 27, 2022
5 photos
This is one of the many times I've visited this restaurant. Typically, I come here to get macaroons when visiting Cali.

Ambiance: This restaurant is massive! There is a louder talking level and very soft music playing. There is so much going on in the restaurant: full bar, restaurant, coffee bar, and a dessert area. The environment is very lively and aesthetic to the eye.

Service: I ordered my desserts at the register and the workers are not that knowledgeable about the desserts they are selling. I tried asking for suggestions and they could not explain to me the desserts they suggested.

Seating: There is plenty of seating since the place is so big! There is bar seating, table/chairs near the dessert area, and a whole restaurant area.

Celebration: This dessert looked really cute so I had to give it a try. I liked the mini vanilla macaroon that came on top of it. The dessert itself was a light vanilla pudding. with white cake and a strawberry filling. It tasted pretty good and I like how the strawberry complements the vanilla flavor.

Chocolate Raspberry: This dessert did not taste good at all! It was a really rich dark chocolate flavor with a almost sour raspberry flavor inside. I could not eat more than one bite as the dark chocolate was too overpowering.

Overall, the pastries and cakes are mediocre and overhyped. I'd say the variety of macaroons and cute packaging is the best part of coming here!

I will start off by saying that I had been a big fan of theirs until the following happened.

Keep reading to see how it turned out!

I had pre-ordered a cake online as well as a goodie for my boyfriend's birthday. I figured I would splurge a little as we previously enjoyed their cakes by the slice.

I decided I would order their guava yuzu cake - The Bella!!!!

I figured rather than gamble my time to see if it was available or sold out, I would order online which was placed 2 days before the actual pick up day aka birthday date.

I hadn't had any problems with cakes or anything as friends and I have ordered ahead of time.

I arrived early in the morning and was super excited to have my order fulfilled. I, then, decided to stay for brunch as well. The box was never moved as I was careful to carry and place the box down as needed.

We proceeded to order the following:
Spinach & Goat Cheese Omelette - it was decent. Very filling and it paired nicely with the side salad it comes with.
Carbonara - The bucatini was a small miss. It was a bit too al dente. The flavors were pretty spot on.
Croque Madame - good choice! Very filling to say the least.

We didn't order any desserts as I had ordered a cake - which he wasn't privy to.

Once we got home and I unpacked, I was shocked by the fact that my cake was far from perfect... It was poorly packed or it was shaken prior to packing for pick up. I was beyond mortified! Sadly, there were no available Patisserie managers available for the weekend... Really?!?!?!?!?!?!

I wrote an email and sent in a picture right away in hopes of having someone contact me. :/

I called in and was told that I wouldn't receive a response until Monday. All I wanted was to have a perfect cake for my boyfriend's birthday! I mean let's be honest, BL cakes are not cheap!

When I called, it was even suggested that the cake moved because of poor movement on my end. I'll have you know, I made sure to not move the box at all... However, I should have checked the cake prior to leaving BL!

Fortunately for me, my sister in law helped me with the cake, so we still consumed it after we had to modify the cake in order to restore it.

Needless to say, once Monday came, I was offered a refund due to the mishap from Saturday. It was super nice of them to go above and beyond.

I do wish their Raspberry Chocolate was available in a bigger size rather than just by the slice! It would be a game changer!

The (imperfect) Bella

Bottega Louie is truly a one stop does it all kind of location.

First - the location and ambiance is simply flat out impressive. Rather Sunday brunch or a late night dessert late in the evening there's something for everyone. The staff are amazing, delightful and knowledgeable about the menu.

But how did it taste?

Bread - unless you're starving allow it to be an accessory on your table. It's not delicious, it's cold and it's not worth the work.

Beignet's - the macaroons are life but at the end of the day their beignets are donuts. Not Café du Ma and not even close to the Grand Luxe Cafe. What I will say is that it was warm, I enjoyed the pink dusted sugar detail and the raspberry purée was amazing.

Lobster Hash - this dish is nearly a 4 star review by itself. Hash brown potatoes (fluffy not crunch) surrounded by a pool of lobster bisque under a scrambled eggs (normally poached) topped with arugula with a hefty lobster tail cascading down the side. Every bite was better than before. The only issue was that the bisque was room temp.

Sammy B.
Elite 26
Whittier, CA
22 Dessert reviews
Sep 16, 2023
10 photos
Bottega Louie feels like how it feels to walk into a Hermes or Louis Vuitton: bougie. The air is filled with energy matched with this atmosphere of excitement for the food and undeniable amounts of photos ready to be taken. The interior design is clean white with elements of gold and wood to accent such pristine decor. If they were hanging clothes next to each table and fashionable bags on the walls you would think you were dining in a Hermes or Louis Vuitton. Large windows bring in extra light along with the gaze of both the envious and curious walking by. The interior could be placed in any modern home spread or fashion magazine and it would make sense it made it there.

The service is on point. Once you get seated the waiters will come by to ask for your drinks along with giving insight to the specials and favorites of the restaurant. If you haven't done research yet you can always look around as both the Hollywood elite and the common man will have faces of excitement when the food arrives. Bottega Louie is also known for their brunches so even better if you arrived at such hours.

You cannot gain notoriety in the food world, much less Los Angeles, unless your food makes a statement and that's what Bottega Louie does: consistently delicious food done simply but with an air of classiness. But what does that actually mean? Let's dissect some food to figure that out.

Let's look at an appetizer that feels familiar but spins it in a different direction. At Bottega Louie they've created a masterpiece of food utilizing the umami flavors of a mushroom along with the familiarity of fries. The Portobello fries are large cut slices of portobello mushrooms in the shape of giant wedges. They are fried golden and topped with parsley and parmesan cheese. They come with an aioli to dip with and these things are phenomenal. Though fried the mushrooms aren't dripping in oil. Though a mushroom these won't be soggy to the bite. With the sharpness of the cheese, the creaminess of the aioli, and the earthy, almost meaty flavor of the mushroom this appetizer is highly elevated in all regards.

How about something sweet? Bottega Louie knows all about sweets but incorporates balance. The French Toast here is a must try. Using a pain de mie bread (common type of white or brown bread used in France with a soft, thin crust) these thick cut slices contain so many different flavors all telling a cohesive story. You have a slight savory flavor from the egg base, a citrus component from the fresh berries and compote, richness from the buttered sliced almonds, and the blast of sweetness from maple syrup to drizzle on the side. All different notes creating a symphony to this breakfast aria.

What of savory dishes? So many to choose from. You can have their Croque Madame, which for those who never have had one before, is like an elevated ham sandwich with a poached/over easy egg on top and béchamel sauce. The Calamari Fritti are lightly fried bouncy pieces of circular squid, a delight for both flavors and mouth feels for those who enjoy texture. And of course, the pizzas. They are phenomenal. All pizzas are cooked over an open fire in a wooden oven and you get that nice smokey flavor along with the char at the edges. They have a variety of different types ranging from the classics such as the Margherita pizza to something with more kick like the Calabrese pizza which has Calabrese salami, fennel confit, pickled Fresno chiles, fresh mozzarella, Pecorino Romano, and Malibu honey. At times they will also have speciality pizzas, for example I had one with their homemade sausage, shaved truffles, and over easy egg. It was so indulgent. Pro-tip: if you do get the pizzas let them rest when they arrive for at least five minutes. Otherwise, you'll have scorching hot breath from these fiery pizzas.

Lastly, you cannot forget the patisserie at the front of the restaurant. No reservations needed to check these well crafted pastries. Not only will each one be a visual delight but the flavors match swimmingly. They are most known for their array of different macrons and when you see them in person you'll understand why. When you buy them they have well designed boxes to wrap them in, making them a fantastic gift to someone or yourself. Rave over their glossy finishes for their cakes and the bountiful layers in their croissants.

Bottega Louie is an experience all in itself. Elevated dining is an understatement to this experience. Wrapped with the luxury of its atmosphere to the different levels of flavors it's no surprise why people from all backgrounds enjoy this restaurant. If you want to as well, make sure you bring your appetite, your camera, and a fat wallet.

I've lived in LA for over a decade and have heard positive things about Bottega Louie but never had the chance eat here until recently. Walked away pleasantly surprised!

Came in around 8:30 on a Friday without a reservation but was able to easily get a table for two. It's a huge and beautiful space with tall ceilings, chic furniture and an enticing bakery areas as soon as you walk in. This place is really nice on the eyes.

The menu is relatively simple, yet elevated Italian fare. Prices average from $20-$40 for most items and the food was elevated enough to feel like the prices were reasonably justified.

Calamari - not the best I've ever had but very well executed, classic preparation.

Truffle Arancini - could have used a little more truffle flavor but these were delicious. Definitely worth a try if you like traditional arancini and want a little twist.

Bianco Pizza - really good. Crust is crispy and airy. Toppings were on point. A little salty but all the more delicious for it.

Drinks - can't remember the names but both cocktails we tried were solid and balanced.

Overall had a really nice experience here. Wasn't the best meal I've ever had but everything was good enough for a 5 star review. Waitstaff was super attentive and friendly. Worth a try!

Fried Calamari

Bottega Louie (I believe) is the highest rated yelped restaurant in LA, so I definitely needed to try them out!

Reading other reviews, it seems like Bottega Louie is packed during peak hours. We ended up stopping by after visitng the Broad museum, so I believe we ate close to 3PM. The restaurant was about 25% full, and we were sat immediately.

Parking is the biggest pain here. Luckily I went back to "feed" the meter, since when I tried to add another 1.5 hours, it wouldn't let me, since it capped during rush hour. I made many loops and wasn't able to find street parking. Opted to park at the parking garage closest to Bottega Louie, and spent maybe $8-10 for an hour-ish.

You definitely get the boujee feel right when you walk in. Everything feels clean and pristine. We ended up ordering the Arugula salad and Calabrese pizza. We were also served complimentary bread.

All in all, food was great. For the price tag and the parking issues, had to ding a star. Not sure if value was there, but still a great spot for good food!

They come in a cute little box which costs a little more but its so pretty who wouldn't want to open it and be surprised that theres macaroons! So basically, it'll make the perfect gift to brighten up someones day just cuz, or bdays etc.

They also serve food here! Ill come back to try their food and ofc pick up some macaroons to go! They have so many pastries, and desserts, and cakes and they looked tasty!

Ron R.
Elite 26
Edgewater, NJ
15 Dessert reviews
Sep 21, 2023
Welcome to Hollywood. Or West Hollywood anyway. We were here for dinner and a special evening to celebrate. We were here a few days before to pick up a pretzel and some pastries. These were excellent. Now we are here for dinner.

And that was some dinner. We sat at the table and met the lovely assistant to help us through the evening. She was very helpful and friendly. We decided to start with our cocktails. A good choice at a fair price was offered. The wine was a good selection to enjoy from both Napa and over in France or Italy and more. This was brought to us along with fresh bread to try as we prepared to order. The bread was ideal. Nice and firm, but soft inside. It was very fresh. An ideal offer as we waited. We even could order two rounds of the bread which was perfect.

We made our order and then waited. The empty location then slowly filled up with more and more guests for the evening. They were all attractive and truly part of the scene. They fit the place. Knew what to eat. And how to enjoy where they were.

Our orders started with salads. These were very fresh and enjoyable. I could have made a meal from the salad by itself. It was plentiful and tasty. The arrangement was ideal. It was the "look" that made it attractive to have.

Next arrived our meals. It was a variety we selected. From a beef rid to chicken parmesan to salmon to pasta. All were different and plentiful as well.

My pasta was nice and different. Having spent several weeks in Rome in May I knew what I expected. This was nice. A bit al dente but a good sauce and fresh pasta to have. The chicken parm was a bit dry. The sauce was good. There was no side such as pasta or vegetable. It was strictly the chicken. The rib was good and tender served on mashed potatoes. The salmon was medium and had its few sides.

Deseret is a selection of pastry and macaroons. It was also very fresh and enjoyable after a graceful meal.

You are not rushed when you are here. Dinner took a few hours to enjoy as you talked and could watch the crowd there. The prices are what you expect. A dinner for 6 does cost money. So expect that to happen. This is more high-end. The service is the best.The team is excellent and proper. They do everything for you before you even expect it. They are there to help and very nice. Our comments to the managers and the team.

Bottega Louie is a wonderful place to visit. You can give it a try. It is in a wonderful area of West Hollywood. It has the right crowd. It has good food. It is a great team. And for an experience it is a place to go. As the old saying goes: "bring your pocketbook". You will need it.

Meanwhile we were entertained by the chefs at the pizza oven, tossing the dough.

The ceilings are 2 stories high so it's very echoey in the restaurant making it hard to have conversation. But the design of the place is lovely including the bathrooms.

We were offered chocolate soufflé for dessert but decided on trying the macarons instead. Raspberry, espresso, and strawberry were increasingly flavorful and delicious in that order. I can understand why people stop by just for dessert!

Megana V.
Elite 26
New York, NY
52 Brunch reviews
Nov 16, 2022
9 photos
After seeing all the hype about their macarons I had to give them a try! We also decided to check out the brunch here. Here's what we got:

- margherita pizza: good pizza, then and crispy but also chewy crust!
- Le pancake: super fluffy and not eggy tasting even though it was a soufflé pancake. It is large and can be shared with 3-4 people. It is quite sweet as well but enjoyed the strawberries and fresh cream!
- truffle fries: basically large chunks of bartered portabella mushrooms, the batter and mushroom don't really congeal well together but the taste was good, esp with the aioli!

The macarons were great! The shell was crispy but chewy. We let it sit out for a while so the cream was too soft but that was on us. Flavors were pretty good!

Overall, I'm glad I gave this place a try. Everything was good, but a bit pricey. Probably wouldn't come here often just bc nothing was super unique but the quality of everything was good!

After completing an escape room in the area we decided to come here for some late lunch. When we arrived around 2PM on a Saturday, we asked for a table of 3 and we're sat almost immediately. The tables are quiet small and we were not large individuals. They have appetizer bread which was shared on the tables. The server was alright.

We ordered the portobello fries, the bingets, and the pancake. We all shared the 3 items. The bingets were fluffy and the raspberry sauce was a perfect balance. The pancake was fluffy and the whipped cream was for sure freshly and homemade whipped. The mushroom fries however were not that great. It's basically a whole slice of portobello mushroom with batter. The batter was not that great for us. It had a thicker and more tough outer layer which seemed kinda stale. The pesto dipping sauce however was tasty.

The restaurant itself was quite loud, which music and tons of customers talking over each other. At one point, a male customer was yelling across the room. So if you're looking for quiet and intimate, maybe not this place.

I walked in and found out this place is combination of 3 -- dessert, bar and Italian restaurant. And I got seated right away (around 5pm) and the whole restaurant got packed around 6:30ish. Ordered old fashion( really delicate cocktail here, strong and pure whisky with taste buds awaken lemon ) it was just right.
Also got the pizza, fresh made with luxury portion of grind garlic. If you are not garlic type of person, be alert.
Of course, you cannot walk out of the restaurant without ordering dessert, mine is chocolate soufflé--- perfect , texture , presentation and taste .
Meanwhile , the atmosphere is elegant Italian style. High ceiling with white paint throughout, all the hardware speaks for the quality. Great for a special event or friends night out. The only problem I experienced was the noise when the restaurant is packed. I guess that is what called night life .
Overall, Highly recommended !

This place is too glorified for me

I went because of all the pictures of this beautiful bakery. Ive seen the macaroons and old bank style building everywhere.

The cakes and pastries were absolutely beautiful but NOT worth anything over $10. $16 on average for a pastry is absurd even if they are intricately made. $6 for a croissant??? When eating it, it was super super dry. The honeycombing of the dough was was too much and had barely any layering which makes it seem so cheap and badly prepared.

The macaroons as well cost $3 each which is actually mind boggling.

I feel as if this place is a tourist trap. The concept of the patisserie and restaurant is really nice and seeing all the chefs around has a nice welcoming vibe yet paying well over $30 for just 2 things should not be normal.

Giving it 3 stars simply because the pastries itself were prepared and displayed very well and the chefs did amazing!

Husband and I shared the lemon tart and gifted the coffee caramel to my mom. The coffee caramel made my mom's day. Lol.

Charlie Y.
San Jose, CA
3 Dessert reviews
Mar 1, 2024

I've been here twice now and both times I had a decent experience. They started us off with complimentary bread and it was solid. We also ordered their tuna tartar and their brussel sprouts with walnuts as appetizers. The tuna tartar was mediocre but the brussel sprouts were surprisingly really good. I would definitely recommend giving that one a try.

We ordered the Bianco pizza to share as a table and it was definitely the best thing we got there. It's a white sauce pizza with lots of ricotta and was super creamy. Their bolognese pasta was recommended by our waiter and it was decent but for the price, I don't think I would recommend it. The salmon was also alright but also didn't impress that much.

After dinner, we walked over to their pastries section and got an assortment of their macrons which were really good. I would recommend trying at least 1 or 2 if you go.

Overall, Bottega Louis had a really nice ambience which is perfect for a date night or event. The staff were all great and the food was good too. While it isn't cheap, I don't think the food is as expensive as other Italian restaurants in the area.

Their salmon Benedict is not as good as people say in my opinion. The reason is the smoked salmon is super salty and anything else on the plate is plain. They might think is a good balance but it will be nicer if they can add some flavor to the veggies and do less sodium to the salmon. Also is a little weird to have hash browns on the bottom, instead of toast. It could be crunchy on the bottom and soggy on the top if they used toast under. I used to come here a lot for their chocolate bars, but never really dined here until recently.

We got garage parking in the parking structure across Grand Ave. I believe it's $7 all day, at least on the weekend. The interior is very bright and definitely has an upscale feel.

We had a decent sized group so we ordered multiple items on the weekend brunch menu:
- French toast: the toast had a soft interior and the fruit was fresh. Other than that, just your standard French toast.
- Lobster hash: the lobster tasted really good and the sauce was not bad either
- Portobello fries: really good and pairs perfectly with the aioli sauce. You might want to eat this one quickly or they might get stuck together
- Calamari fritti: good crisp. Also basically just standard calamari
- Croque madame: tasted like an ordinary sandwich.
- Calabrese pizza: each bite has a very fresh taste and also was a bit with the honey
- Tartufo pizza: definitely recommend this one. You can smell the truffle on the melted cheese and you can certainly taste it as well. The egg garnished on the pizza is also nice and adds a nice color to it. My only complaint about this one is the crust is a bit chewy and could be fluffier in a sense.

We also got two cocktails: Juicy and Bellini. Both were nice and sweet, but I would say the Juicy tasted slightly better for us.

Overall, food is a bit on the pricey side, but the quality is pretty much there. Service and ambiance add more value to justify the price too.

My daughter reeeally wanted macarons on a recent trip to DTLA for the Emo Orchestra. I am not a fan of macarons, but I decided to take her. We attempted to stop in before the show, circled the block several times and wasn't able to find parking. We went on with our night and attempted again after the show. After a few more trips around the block, we finally found parking a block over. We parked and walked to the location. As we walked up, we noticed the lights over the dessert/macaron area were off and almost everything was put away. Everywhere I looked online says hours are until 12am, but it turns out that the dessert counter closes at 10pm. It was now 10:18pm. We were so sad. While talking to the guy who was closing up the desert counter, he mentioned that we could walk over to the bar and purchase a variety box of random macarons, a blind box, per se. We decided to go for it since we were already there and my daughter really wanted to try them. The 2 flavors that she really wanted to try (Espresso and Ultimate Chocolate) were in the box! She was so excited. She said they were really good and everything that she wanted. I decided to take a nibble of the Mango flavor...and oh my goodness was it goooood! I tried little bites of the other flavors and they were also very delicious. The best macarons that I have ever tried. I still dream about that Mango macaron...I'll be stopping in again soon. For sure.

After getting an email from Yelp CM, RE: top 50 most reviewed restaurant and eateries on Yelp. I Had Bottega Louie bookmarked and many others on the list and it was perfect since I was going to Orange County for the weekend. Over 17,000 + reviews and #1 on the list, must visit for me. I was excited, and asked my wonderful hubby the Uber driver to take me. I even gave up my favorite donut spot out at Newport for Bottega. Actually I cheated and had a Krispy Kreme donut along the way.

Important first time info. for newbies like me. No reservations, walk ins only, valet parking only, and the long line in front is not for the restaurant, it is for the bakery ( I was in the wrong line but it worked out as I wanted to get desserts while the other half went to get a table) Expect a long wait and also to show proof of vaccination as we had to show ours during the month of March. Also expect noise from the busy crowd, if you want quiet this is not a place for you.

Bottega Louie is super nice, huge restaurant, a gourmet market, patisserie and cafe. Located in Downtown Los Angeles. W/only one hour on the meter we were limited on time. My hubby did not want to walk back to car to feed the meter and walk back, ( our mistake, next time we will have to plan better) We had to rush and ended up only getting and sharing the lobster hash $29., which was recommended by our server. The lobster hash was amazingly delicious, I loved how the entree was put together. The hash was nice and crispy, loved popping the egg yolk, and the sauce was so good with complimentary bread, I also loved eating the bread with the egg yolk and I saved the the lobster, the best for last.

For desserts, I got the 6 piece macaroon in a heart shaped box ($20.) and original beignets to go. The flavors of macaroon all sound so good and it was hard to choose. The beignets, my opinion taste more like a malasada (Portuguese fried dough) because of the sugar, did not come with the powdered sugar. I didn't know there were more pastries on the other end, wanted to try the Kouign but didn't want to wait in line. next time.

All in all, we had a great first time experience at the most reviewed restaurant on Yelp and I plan to be back again soon. This time I have plans for more lobster hash and def. try the lunch items. We will be back soon, Bottega's already on my list for my upcoming trip.
Totally recommend, just be prepared since it is a very popular restaurant.
