--- WIKIPEDIA + GEMINI SYNTHESIS ---

Zaryadye Park emerges as a meticulously sculpted urban landscape, a testament to contemporary design's ability to weave complex programs into a seemingly organic form. Spanning nearly 78,000 square meters, its immediate presence adjacent to Red Square establishes a grand civic scale, yet the park cleverly mitigates this through a series of "facilities hidden under the landscape." This integrated approach means nature and architecture are not merely juxtaposed but act as a singular entity, creating a sequence of discovery rather than a direct, overwhelming spectacle. The overarching concept of layering, particularly the division into four distinct climatic zones – forest, steppe, tundra, and floodplains – suggests a journey that descends from northeast to southwest, implying a varied sensory progression from one ecological condition to another, each potentially offering unique thermal and acoustic signatures as visitors traverse the undulating terrain.

Material textures play a crucial role in defining these sensory experiences. The Soaring Bridge, for instance, offers a striking architectural gesture: a thin, airy V-shaped structure cantilevering 70 meters over the Moscow River without a single support. Its bearing structure of concrete is juxtaposed with decorative metal elements and a warm wooden deck, inviting touch and emphasizing its role as a grand public promenade. This combination creates a unique acoustic environment, where the gentle thrum of pedestrian movement on wood might contrast with the ambient sounds of the river below, all while offering expansive views of the Kremlin. The sense of suspension, both visually and physically, for the 3,000 to 4,000 people it can accommodate, is heightened by the outward extension above the water, fostering a communal awe balanced by the practical presence of high fences.

Thermal conditions within the park are remarkably diverse, offering dramatic shifts in sensation. The Ice Cave stands out as a stark thermal counterpoint to Moscow's climate, a stylized pavilion where temperatures are consistently maintained between -2°C and -12°C. The formation of this space with 70 tons of water results in an interior of "natural white colour," suggesting a visually cool and acoustically dampened environment where sounds might be absorbed by ice, creating an eerie quietness. This extreme chill contrasts sharply with the open-air climatic zones, where the sun's path across the sky throughout the day would generate a dynamic interplay of light and shade, affecting the perceived warmth or coolness of the forest, steppe, tundra, and floodplains respectively.

Water, both as a natural element and a constructed feature, is integral to Zaryadye's performance. The park’s location on the Moscow River provides a constant backdrop, with the Soaring Bridge extending dramatically over its surface, directly connecting visitors to the vastness of the waterway. Beyond this, the "floodplains" climatic zone explicitly integrates water into the landscape design. Even the 70 tons of water required for the Ice Cave and the pebbles in the amphitheater paths, designed as a drainage system, underscore a thoughtful engagement with this element, hinting at specific acoustic qualities – perhaps the gentle trickling of runoff or the crisp silence within the frozen cavern.

Social dynamics are carefully choreographed through a variety of programmed spaces. The concert hall, housed within a hill with only its glass facade revealed, offers a grand scale for gatherings, hosting symphonic music, pop, and jazz. Adjacent to it, the large open amphitheater, accommodating 1500 spectators, is covered by a "glass bark" dome, providing shade and rain protection, demonstrating a pragmatic yet elegant solution for outdoor events. Its stepped landscape design, wooden spots, and pebble paths create a convivial atmosphere for communal viewing, while the smaller amphitheater caters to more intimate groups of 400 for screenings and lectures. These spaces are designed to encourage different forms of collective experience, from the formal reverence of a concert to the relaxed enjoyment of an outdoor film.

The sequence of experience through Zaryadye is designed to be immersive and varied. Moving from the expansive natural zones, visitors can delve into the "hidden" programmatic spaces. The Media Center, covering 8500 square meters, offers a journey through Russian history and nature via "concave" and "cylindrical" screens, transporting viewers on "dynamic platforms." This highly controlled, multi-sensory sequence within a darkened hall contrasts sharply with the open, flowing experience of the park, creating moments of intense focus and narrative immersion, distinct from the broader social interactions outside.

Shade and light are expertly manipulated to enhance comfort and aesthetic appeal. The "glass bark" over the large amphitheater is a key example, offering diffused light and protection from the elements, allowing performances to continue regardless of weather. The "facilities hidden under the landscape" inherently create sheltered, cooler zones, contrasting with the open sun-drenched areas of the park's terraces. The Soaring Bridge, while open to the sky, creates its own shadow play over the river, and its sheer span might offer a sense of exposure balanced by the structural enclosure.

Scale is continuously modulated, from the panoramic vista offered by the Soaring Bridge to the intimate, contained environments like the Ice Cave or the media center's cylindrical screen, which virtually transports viewers. This strategic shift in scale ensures that visitors encounter a spectrum of experiences, preventing monotony. The design’s success lies in its ability to manage large crowds (up to 4,000 on the bridge, 1,500 in the amphitheater) while still offering moments of individual contemplation within the diverse climatic zones or programmed exhibits.

At different times of day, Zaryadye Park would perform distinctively. By day, the open climatic zones would be bathed in natural light, with shadows shifting across the descending terraces, highlighting the varied textures of forest, steppe, tundra, and floodplain. The "glass bark" would filter sunlight into the amphitheater, creating a pleasant environment for daytime events. As dusk falls, the park's artificial lighting would transform the landscape, drawing attention to architectural features like the luminous glass facade of the concert hall and potentially enhancing the dramatic view of the Kremlin from the Soaring Bridge. The enclosed media center, concert hall, and ice cave, being temperature and light controlled, would function consistently, offering year-round experiences that transcend the diurnal cycle of the open park, positioning Zaryadye as a dynamic urban amenity that evolves with the hours.

Based on the provided satellite imagery and map overlay of Zaryadye Park, Moscow, the following professional analysis is conducted:

### Landscape Architecture and Urban Planning Analysis: Zaryadye Park

**1. SITE CONTEXT:**
Zaryadye Park is strategically situated within a highly significant historical, cultural, and civic urban fabric in central Moscow. To its immediate **north and northwest**, it is bordered by prominent landmarks including Red Square, St. Basil's Cathedral, and the historical Ulitsa Varvarka, which features structures like the Chambers of Romanov Boyars and the Church of the Great Martyr George. This indicates a deeply rooted historical and cultural presence, drawing significant tourist and local foot traffic. To its **west**, the park abuts the eastern wall of the Kremlin, including the Spasskaya Bashnya and Beklemishevskaya Tower, reinforcing its connection to Russia's governmental and historical core. The **south** boundary is defined by the Moskva River and the Moskvoretskaya Embankment, establishing a crucial waterfront presence and integrating the park into the city's major aquatic thoroughfare and associated infrastructure (Bolshoy Moskvoretsky Most). The **east** side appears to transition into a more general urban fabric, likely comprising mixed-use or commercial developments, though specific details are less visible. Overall, the park occupies a premium urban site, acting as a green oasis and a contemporary intervention amidst an iconic historical setting and major urban infrastructure.

**2. GEOMETRIC LOGIC:**
The park's primary organizational shape, while generally elongated and rectangular along the river, is characterized by a sophisticated and predominantly organic, fluid geometry internally. Strict grids are notably absent within the park's green spaces and pedestrian networks. Instead, winding, curvilinear paths dominate, weaving through extensive softscapes and creating a sense of natural flow. These paths often converge and diverge in fragmented intersections rather than simple orthogonal crossings.

Distinct programmatic zones within the park exhibit variations in their internal geometric logic:
*   Areas around the **Media Center** appear to have a more defined, possibly radial or concentric pattern of paths and planting, suggesting a planned hub.
*   The vicinity of the **Concert Hall "Zaryad'ye"** features a blend of the architectural form of the hall itself (appearing as a large, distinct structure) and surrounding softscape integrated with curvilinear walkways and terraced elements.
*   The overall layout prioritizes a naturalistic, flowing landscape experience, contrasting with the more rigid geometries of the adjacent urban grid and historical fortifications.

**3. PROGRAMMATIC ZONES:**
An estimation of the internal park area's programmatic zones reveals a clear emphasis on natural and semi-natural landscapes:

*   **Softscape (Trees, Lawns, Plantings):** Approximately **60-70%**. This is the dominant land cover. The park features extensive tree canopies, varying from dense clusters to more open groves, alongside large expanses of lawns and intricate planting beds. The organic path network is deeply embedded within these softscape elements, providing immersive green experiences.
*   **Hardscape (Paving, Plazas, Structures):** Approximately **20-30%**. This includes the footprints of significant structures like the Media Center, Concert Hall, Zaryadye Gastronomic Center, and Voskhod. It also encompasses the network of primary and secondary paved pedestrian paths, smaller plazas, and transition zones at park entrances. The large paved area of Ploshchad Vasilyevskiy Spusk immediately west of the park, though technically external, functions as a major hardscaped gateway.
*   **Blue Space (Internal Water Features):** Approximately **1-5%**. While the Moskva River is a defining external blue space, internal water features appear limited to smaller, ornamental ponds or reflective pools, possibly integrated near the Media Center or Concert Hall, contributing minorly to the overall park area.

**4. SPATIAL RELATIONSHIPS:**

*   **Edge to City:**
    *   **Northern Edge (Ulitsa Varvarka):** This edge presents a relatively defined, linear interface with the historical street. While there are clear pedestrian access points and visual connections to adjacent historical buildings, the transition appears somewhat abrupt in places, suggesting a boundary rather than a seamless merge.
    *   **Western Edge (Ploshchad Vasilyevskiy Spusk / Kremlin):** This is a highly permeable and ceremonial edge. The park seamlessly flows into the vast Ploshchad Vasilyevskiy Spusk, which acts as a grand civic plaza connecting to Red Square and the Kremlin. This design creates a powerful visual and pedestrian corridor, making the park a direct extension of Moscow's most iconic public space.
    *   **Southern Edge (Moskvoretskaya Embankment / Moskva River):** This is a highly integrated and performative edge. The Moskvoretskaya Embankment provides a strong linear connection, allowing for waterfront promenade and access. The "Floating Bridge" dramatically extends the park experience over the river, transforming what could be a mere boundary into an active, immersive blue space experience, offering panoramic views and creating a strong visual and physical link with the water.
*   **Focal Points / Pavilions:**
    *   **Media Center:** Positioned centrally, this structure functions as a key programmatic and architectural focal point, surrounded by open spaces and radiating paths.
    *   **Concert Hall "Zaryad'ye":** A significant cultural anchor, its distinctive architectural form serves as a major landmark and a destination within the eastern section of the park.
    *   **Floating Bridge:** This is arguably the most dramatic and iconic architectural intervention. Its unique cantilevered design extends over the Moskva River, acting as a major experiential focal point, offering visitors unique vantage points and an elevated promenade.
    *   **Zaryadye Gastronomic Center and Voskhod:** These functional pavilions provide amenities and contribute to the park's vibrancy as destinations for leisure and dining.
    *   **St. Basil's Cathedral:** Although external, its proximity and iconic status make it a powerful visual focal point when viewed from the western parts of the park, framing key views and reinforcing the park's historical context.
    *   Smaller, designed landscape elements, such as distinct planting patterns, terraced areas, and gathering spaces, also act as micro-focal points throughout the park's organic network.

      "text": "We visited in April ,hotel and staff are all amazing !! You won’t go wrong with this hotel so helpful and such a pleasant stay!!Dear ben c,Thank you for choosing 1926 Le Soleil Hotel & Spa!It brings us immense joy to read such a positive review about your stay with us in April.       "tripDate": "April 2025",
      "tripType": "Traveled with family"

    {
      "title": "Honest review",
      "text": "I feel very disappointed after staying here for 3 nights and I can completely agree with the others who state that they have been mislead. The hotel is not on the seafront, not like the pictures advertised of the beach club. The hotel entrance is down a side street a few streets back from the sea front. The room we had was upgraded to a superior double. I would have expected a normal double to be of this size and standard in a 4 star hotel. It was basic however, on the plus side, the bed was big and comfy. The room was cleaned daily and we got fresh towels when we requested them.There was a selection of items at breakfast - cooked and continental which were regularly topped up. The orange juice was fresh and they had a selection of fresh fruit too.The bar served a large selection of premium drinks and the cocktails looked nice and good value with a happy hour. There was building work going on outside the hotel which was noisy but I think it could have been worse if we were on a lower level floor. The spa was a big let down, there was about 4 loungers and a few hard chairs. There was only one jet in the hydro pool which worked as the other two were broken. The sauna and steam room were small but adequate. The whole spa needs a massive revamp, it looks a bit grubby especially around the pool area. Don’t believe the reviews about Prosecco on arrival as the hotel does not offer this.The location of the hotel is good for catching the ferry to Valletta but overall, I think there is other hotels in the area which are more premium class for around the same price.",
      "rate": 3.0,
      "tripDate": "March 2025",
      "tripType": "Traveled as a couple"
    },

Book early to secure places on trips and the rooftop pool amenities",

      "title": null,
      "text": "Excellent Hotel and great staff. Nice size standard  room. Small balcony but didn’t use much and a bit overlooked but not an issue to us.     {
      "title": "Perfect stay!",
      "text": "Everything i have to say about this hotel is positive! From the friendly/helpful staff, to the complimentary sunset cruise.     {
      "title": "Excellent hotel in the best location in Malta",
      "text": "Excellent hotel with reception and other very beautiful dependencies. Great maintenance, impeccable cleaning and very kind staff. Located 150mt from the ferry to Valeta, descending a light slope. It has a spa with included access (part treatments) in the same building, a very well equipped gym right in front with also included access and a great beach club about 150mts from the hotel (functioning only in the warmer months). On the rooftop there is a terrace with swimming pool, sun loungers and couples' cabins, and reservation (free) is required in advance. Rooms and spacious bathrooms. I stayed in a room with a balcony with 2 chairs and noise-proof door, which ensures a peaceful sleep, even though the place is quiet.The room has a very comfortable king-size bed, a sofa bed with TV in front but sideways to the bed. There is a desk with chair and center table. 2-door cabinet with safe. The box is wide and has good water flow. The wi-fi signal is strong and very fast. The breakfast is rich, varied and all very tasty.It is a great choice in the best location of Malta (Sliema), away from the nightly bustle of St. Julians and the moisture of Valeta, but close to both.",
      "rate": 5.0,
      "tripDate": "June 2025",
      "tripType": "Traveled as a couple"
    },
    {
      "title": "Great Choice Despite Minor Issues",
      "text": "Overall very pleased we spent a week at this hotel. The location is ideal with easy walking to the shops, restaurants and ferries. The staff at reception were always attentive and friendly. The breakfast had a large selection and of good quality, I liked the fact you were greeted on entry and advised which table to use rather than wandering around trying to find one. We rarely had to wait for the lift. I was amazed that a complimentary tour was available in a historic bus with a trip to the Blue Grotto, Dingli Cliffs and Mdina. The beachside club was excellent apart from one staff member who was aggressive and rude when we had the temerity to drink water from a bottle not purchased at the facility, we didn’t know the rule but the message should have been delivered in a different manner. The bedroom furniture was far from standard and ideal with no wardrobes or drawers to put things away. A poorly thought out choice. There was lots of noise from very early in the morning from the updating of the building opposite including, contrary to my travel company info, Sunday. Ear plugs certainly helped and as we were advised before going it didn’t bother me as out all day. I recommend two trips, the Tuk Tuk tour of Gozo was awesome and you were picked up close to the hotel and The Sunset Cruise to the Blue Lagoon by Hornblower Cruises, much quieter at this time and sunset fantastic. Uber rides are inexpensive compared with the UK and if travelling with Jet2 I would forget the arranged transport (arrived early and got us to the airport too soon after lots of pickups on the way and terrible attitude) and go by Uber or Bolt. Overall loved our holiday in Sliema and very pleased I chose this hotel.",
      "rate": 4.0,
      "tripDate": "June 2025",
      "tripType": "Traveled as a couple"
    },
    {
      "title": "Wow, just wow",
      "text": "What an exceptional hotel. This should definitely be a 5* hotel. From the moment we arrived we just knew this was going to be special. Cold water on arrival. Check in with Francesca was quick and easy with everything explained. No room key, access to room is keypad which is a great idea. The room (Premium suite) was simply stunning, bright, spacious and relaxing. Incredible views of the sea from the balcony.Every single member of the hotel staff were so friendly, attentive and would do anything they could for you, even making sure you had cool water in your room fridge. Rooftop pool was amazing, although getting into warm water for a swim was a little shock (it’s heated only by the sun). You don’t have to leave your sun bed for drinks or food, simply scan the code on your table to order and it arrives. The beach club/infinity pool is not attached to the hotel, it’s across the road off the promenade. It too is stunning, cold and refreshing with unbelievable views across the sea. Spent many hours in the water watching the cruise ships coming in and out. Again, staff here were amazing, attentive and very friendly. I have booked to come back already.",
      "rate": 5.0,
      "tripDate": "June 2025",
      "tripType": "Traveled with family"
    },
    {
      "title": "Clean and comfortable with excellent service",
      "text": "We booked a Junior Suite. Our arrival was all very courteous and pleased to find we had a 10th floor room which gave us panoramic and sea views which we had not completely expected. We had a balcony, plenty long enough, but only around 2ft wide, and no room to walk past  one of the small chairs. There is so much building work going on in the vicinity, I counted 40 tower cranes from our balcony, but work does not start until after 8am and finishes no later than 6pm, so no unwelcome disturbance. We used the roof top pool, although the booking system was not very efficient for this, and everything in the hotel is booked on their app, which they help you set up on your phone when you check in. The roof top pool is quiet, only around a dozen sunbeds, but they are fabulous and comfy and plenty of parasols which staff are happy to put up and adjust for you. Despite the booking system making it appear fully booked, when we were there on 3 occasions , there were no more than 4 other guests. The downside - the pool was too shallow to swim in, and if you want to cool down after roasting in the sun, use the shower, the pool is far too hot, like bath water.We also used the hotel's private water front pool, bar and sunbed area. The pool was great, able to manage a little swim, and  perfect temperature. Sunbeds were good, and parasols again put up by staff. It was much more congested down here , no need to book, just turn up and towels provided. Drinks available any time, just catch the waiter's eyes. Disappointed not to be able to bring own water, it had to be bought from the bar, no outside food or drink could be brought in. My big gripe was loud club disco music - it is not a club 18-30s hotel so why play loud thumping bass rubbish. Fortunately a couple of visits to Le Plage area they had someone playing the music quieter, and although not an Abba aficionado, hearing 4 of their songs in an hour was a distinct improvement.Breakfast was a good spread, with English cooked breakfast, continental meats and cheeses, fruit yoghurts, a choice of pastries and various teas and coffees from a machine. We only had one lunch, taken down at Le Plage, a decent enough burger and chips.Room was spacious, bed and pillows comfortable, a good size wardrobe, with iron and board, we had to ask for more hangers, but ended not unpacking our bags because there was not a single drawer - very disappointing. There was a room safe, and the room was cleaned daily, and also an early evening visit to top up on the complimentary tea and nespresso machine. One complimentary daily bottle of water as tap water not considered good for English palate. air conditioning worked well and not too noisy. Nice big shower, but I dont think the guys that installed it, put on a big enough slope, as I ended having to squeegee the water into the drain with my feet after the shower.We had a great complimentary bus tour visiting 3 locations on the island on a 1960's vintage bus, I was a little disappointed it was not the older vintage bus shown on their promotional pics, but you cant complain about free. I was disappointed that the night after was arrived there was also a free sunset cruise round Valletta harbour, which we were not made aware of, and the reception staff indicated it runs on a Wednesday - however after looking for it on their app it did not appear, and it was only the day before the reception staff told us it was not running that week. So we ended up booking one of the trips through VisitMalta.com.We booked a taxi through the hotel back to the airport, very clean, on time and cost Eur23 for the two of us.At round £200 per night with breakfast it was pricey, and I guess we got what we paid for. I have to admit  that paying that much, I was expecting a more usable balcony, drawers in the room, a less rowdy pool by the sea, and to be able to use water from the room or elsewhere by the pool, and not to have to pay Eur3 for a small bottle. I thought also to qualify as a suite it would be more than an ensuite room with a skinny balcony. On the plus side, there was the complimentary tour, and a panoramic view. Decent location near bus stops and Sliema Ferries with plenty of local restaurants and close to 2 shopping precincts.",

      "tripDate": "June 2025",
      "tripType": "Traveled as a couple"
    },

    {
      "title": "Nice hotel with many additional features",
      "text": "We were here on our honeymoon for a week. This hotel's top features include a Michelin restaurant, a spa, a rooftop terrace with pool and serving, and a beach club. The breakfast was very good, and the drinks in the bar also impeccable. Standard rooms were slightly smaller in size and equipment than we had expected and actually suitable not more than a weekend for a couple. The balcony saved us. The air conditioning only works when someone is in the room and actively starts it, and when the balcony door is closed. The latter was not always quite easy to get (the tired door). I did not expect to see mold in the bathroom when the hotel looks so newly renovated. We had to ask quite a few times for small things that the cleaning staff had forgotten to fill with, but I think they were under pretty much pressure, and we were always brought what we needed to the room. Everyone we met from the hotel staff were friendly and helpful.",
      "rate": 4.0,
      "tripDate": "September 2025",
      "tripType": "Traveled as a couple"
    },
    {
      "title": "Nice hotel, great location and facilities. Has a few issues.",
      "text": "We stayed here for a short break, just 2 adults. Prior to our arrival, the hotel messaged via WhatsApp asking for pre registration and access to their website app where you can book a taxi from the airport to the hotel (it’s €23 through the hotel. Or you can go to the taxi stand in the airport and it is €20). We tried to get an Uber which would have been cheaper but the payment wouldn’t authorise through PayPal, as it was coming up as €13. Maybe Malta doesn’t use PayPal? The hotel entrance is very modern with nice decor and a calming environment. We were greeted by staff, who, throughout the stay, were nothing but friendly and helpful. Our room wasn’t ready when we arrived. Check in is at 3pm and we arrived at about 11:30. We had requested an earlier check in which was approved, subject to availability. We requested this, along with a high floor and sea view, when we made the booking through Booking.com (other booking sites available…). Even though the room wasn’t available, the staff explained that we could still make use of the beach club which is about 100 metres from the hotel whilst we wait for the room. The beach club has its own restaurant and bar area. There are a lot of sun beds available. Parasols also, which you have to ask the staff to put up, down, move around. We were told that you aren’t allowed to take any other food or drinks in from outside and they need to be purchased from their bar. Large water is €4. Cocktails are €11. They have happy hour everyday 4pm-6pm which is a set choice cocktail menu, 2-4-1 on the same cocktail. The food is slightly pricey in the restaurant (brioche ham and cheese toastie with crisps - €14) but tastes nice. The pool area of the beach club is nice. Some maintenance is required on the pool as tiles are missing and the pool floor in places had bits on it, almost like it hadn’t been vacuumed. However, it looks out at the sea and there is access via some steps to the sea if you want to go in. There is no sandy beach in Sliema. It’s all rocks. Beach club opens at 9:30. Closes around 6pm. Once the room was available, you get the access code via the website app. This must be a new system as the doors to the rooms still have locking barrels in them. The doors don’t feel or sound very secure. Each time our neighbour went in and out of their door, it was like they were coming in through our door! The hotel have put a new coded system in but not upgraded the doors. Due to this, the rooms aren’t very sound proof and quite a bit of noise can be heard from outside the room. We booked the superior room which had breakfast included. Quite a bit of space available for us two. Enough hanging and folding space for clothes. Areas to store suitcases. Tea and coffee available in the room and bottle of water, for which, the first one is free but there after you need to pay for another. The shower was small and the water didn’t appear to get that warm. The plastic around the shower door was also damaged so water leaked out into the bathroom quite a lot. The shower also didn’t feel very secure. The sliding doors were wobbling. They came out of their guiders occasionally. The whole unit just needs replacing really. The hotel has Molton Brown shower gel and shampoo on the wall. We ran out of shower gel after the first shower so had to call reception to refill it, someone came straight away to do this.The air con system in the room is good in the sense that it does make the room cool but the conservation/eco system they have in place is ridiculous. The air con only works if all the doors are closed and you are in the room as it is motion activated. So when you want it on through the night, the air con turns off and the switch is too far away from the bed to just reach up and turn it back on. So you have to get up out of bed, in the middle of the night to turn the air con on again. And it appears to only be on for an hour. During our stay, the room was too stuffy and hot to not have the air con on through the night. The air con also doesn’t automatically come back on once you close the balcony doors. So you open the doors and the air con goes off but once you close the doors, you would think it would come back on but it doesn’t. On the topic of balcony doors, one of ours didn’t lock. Luckily we were on the 6th floor so possibly not going to get someone breaking in. However, not really the point. We had to chase maintenance twice to come and have a look at balcony door which resulted in not being able to open the balcony door as we couldn’t lock it again once it had been opened. It just needs replacing. The bed was large and comfy. The hotel have put a mattress topper on it although it was too small for the bed so felt strange having the pillows on the actual mattress and then the rest of your body higher up than your head. Either get a mattress topper that fits or get rid of it. One of the light switches was cracked and broken. The plug socket made my plug extremely hot. The USB charging socket next to the bed didn’t let me use my phone whilst it was charging. I didn’t really trust the electrics in that room in all honestly. I just think for a 4* hotel, it’s not great the amount of things that were broken, not working, not quite up to 4* hotel standard that were in our room. You have access to the gym, which is across the road. We didn’t use it but seemed very modern. One of the staff stated it had only been open 2 months.The website app is quite good. On here, you can make reservation for the rooftop pool, book taxis and get general information. We had to join a waiting list for the rooftop pool area as it only allows a certain amount of guests up there at any one time. There are gazebos available and sun beds with a parasol but you need to reserve your spot well in advance otherwise you are unlikely going to be able to get up there. That being said, we were luckily and just went up one afternoon and asked the staff if any were available (this was about 3:30pm) and a gazebo was available so we were able to have that for the afternoon. Bookings are 9am-1pm and then 2pm-6pm. So if you want to book all day up there, you need to make sure both times on your preferred date is available. Otherwise, you might get asked to move…Breakfast was a choice of pastries, cold meats, salad, fresh fruit, cereal, yoghurt, “full English” type food, toast, tea, coffee and juice. It’s open 7-10:30 weekday and 7-11 weekend. Sometimes the hot food was a bit cold if it had been out for a while. We used the app to book a couple of taxis during our stay. One to the airport from the hotel which was €23. Reception stated that it is room charge but it turns out that it defaults to pay the driver, who does have a card machine within their car. So once we arrived at the airport, we just paid by card in the car. We also booked a taxi to St Julien’s which is €12. However, there doesn’t appear to be a way of booking a taxi for the return journey so we were advised by reception to get a card from the taxi driver and contact them when we want picking up. We just called the hotel again and asked them to contact the taxi company for us instead, thinking it would be easier for payment as it would just go on the room. The taxi booking system seems like it could be easier. Upon our arrival at St Julien’s, the driver expected payment for the journey. We advised them that we would be paying the hotel as we were told by reception staff it is room charge. The driver had to double check with their boss which was fine and all okay. Upon arrival back at the hotel, we made the payment of €12 for the journey and thought nothing more of it. The next day, whilst en-route to the airport to go home, the taxi driver stated they have been informed by the hotel staff that we hadn’t paid for the St Julien’s taxi journey. Yet, when we checked out, we were told everything had been finalised, nothing to pay. If you are going to have a taxi booking service via a hotel website app, just have it that guests pay the hotel and the taxi service invoices the hotel rather than having multiple options to pay. Clearly just gets confusing. All in all, it is a nice hotel with helpful and friendly staff. Nothing seems too much of a problem for them - supplying us with additional beach towels for a boat trip. Lots of facilities available for use. Exclusive use of the beach club, rooftop pool area, wellness options. It’s in a good location with lots of restaurant, bars and shops a 5-10 minute walk down a hill to the harbour front away. However, with the problems and issues stated above, would give this hotel 4 out of 5.",

      "tripType": "Traveled as a couple"
